Dear Eve,

Yesterday the Queen‘s birthday was celebrated in New Zealand – which meant that the kids and adults were home. I think that’s the last long weekend for Walt until October. We celebration the day by placing more trees. (I think we have put in over 50 now (30 of those are for a privacy fence in 5–8 years ) between our house and the one next-door. Houses here are quite close together and you either you either look into someone else’s lounge or kitchen. We are fortunate that we have quite a lot of privacy except one side – so that’s why we are putting a tree line on that side. Also we planted some feijoa (some exotic fruit) trees that do well in sun and wind. Our section looks quite a bit different than it did when we moved in – better we hope!

The kids are back in school for the second trimester. We go to Matthew's school [Wellington College, Wellington]   Thursday night for conference with his teachers. And on Saturday the 14th, we are invited to a party that the parents of one of the students in Matthew’s home room class is giving – just to get to know each other – sounds like a good idea to me since I don’t know any of the kids or parents. Since I work at Kris and Kate ‘s school library and kitchen  , I have met, some of those parents.

We did enjoy our trip to Mount Taranaki  - did you get the card? We had never seen such beautiful birds as we saw at a park in New Plymouth.  I didn’t know anything so intensely colorful existed.

We also took a walk part of the way to the top of Mount Taranaki. Matthew Kate and Kris set the pace of our walk and what the book said would take 2 1/2 to 3 hours, took us 59 minutes 30 seconds. I think the vegetation was lovely but going so fast it was hard to see more than a blur. Kris said at one point in a very surprised voice, “you know I’m actually enjoying this.“ This from someone who usually drags at the rear claiming sore feet, aching head, etc. was pleasant to hear.

Did I tell you that Mary and Jimmy GIll my sister are coming to see us in January. We are looking forward to that. I think we can come there for Christmas 87 – anyway, we hope so after Walt has worked at Data Bank two years, he gets an extra month’s salary and travel vouchers each year. I don’t know what the travel vouchers actually cover, but we (me) are hoping we can swing it - visit Fort Worth, Austin and Wisconsin.

Well, how are you all doing? It seems strange that it’s summer. There are strange that it’s not summer here after all it is June! Did Julie graduate? Did you visit Austin? Did you miss us? We miss having good friends come for the weekend.

I don’t understand what embarrassed you about writing down your birthdays and anniversaries(?) Did you ever see Jerry and Missy? Karen? Rusty? How is he doing with the new wife? With the old wives? I’m working on these needlepoint covers for our dining room chairs – I’m almost finished with the first one – only three to go! Must go – hope to hear from you soon.

Love,

Pat.

Letter's Context

2026-10-30

A nation is a lot of things.
When I was an American, some of those things were family and community and county and state and religion and education and identifications. That plane of abstractions rode, uneasily, in a Maxwellian field of terrain and soil and weather and flower and fauna. Those abstractions and field bobbed in a magnetic field of culture and history and tools of comprehension.

It was different being a Kiwi.
The family of my Father and Mother lived in Niederhochst, Deutschland; community and county and state and religion differed in ways I'd never experienced in 44 years of being an American. But jarringly different were terrain and soil and weather and flower and fauna.

A Polynesian Noah loaded not an ark, but a trimaran with flower and fauna known nowhere else but Aotearoa.  North Island soil simmered with volcanic instability that colored Wellington architecture, both humble and proud. The BNZ tower building in wellington had not only vertical columns, but transverse columns, the better to endure tremblors; our Karori home was wired with both electrical and structural, tensile wiring to better the chances of crawling out of a quake shattered home.

The quick time march up the flanks of Taranaki and the lumpy fields of millenia old massive volcanic bombs on the drive from Palmerston North to New Plymouth park were echt Kiwi.

wrote July 17, 1986 of July winter, job or school, warmer fireplace, Walt & Matt fishing in Cook strait, running injury and driving test. +Context

15 Lynmouth Ave..
Karori, Wellington 5, New Zealand

2114 Tierney Ave.
Ft Worth, TX

Dear Eve,

I wonder when we will begin to associate winter with July! It’s not really freezing cold weather – I think there’s only been one morning below 32°F – but when the Southerlies blow and the rain comes down for days at a time, I think a nice quiet snowstorm would be nice. We do have wonderful views of winter though – in the distance are snow covered mountains [ Remutakas] that can be seen on sunny warm days like today. The warmer days of winter are wonderful and probably outnumbered the rainy cold ones.

Did you have a July 4 party? We had some friends (Argentinians and New Zealanders) over for dinner; I did serve apple pie. It was not really a celebration of independence, just sort of happened on that date.

We haven’t been doing anything exciting lately. I’ve been halfheartedly looking for a part-time job. I’ll either get a job – or go back to school. I’m not highly motivated at the present time, but I am trying a special semester at Victoria University in a couple of weeks[-] will rekindle old fires (they may be out by now). Speaking of fires we finally have a house with a fireplace – but it’s more for show than heat (a black freestanding "designer" one) so we have been looking at more efficient wood heaters – that also looked halfway decent. And we ran across a lady originally from Boston (she married a New Zealander 20 years ago) publishes a paper called “Energy Watch“. We went there to look at a stove “ugly as sin“ they were selling and then started talking about solar energy. She “Molly“ and her husband [a nuclear engineer, educated at UCLA) insisted we stay for lunch we -spent the whole afternoon with them – talking of this and that – they had wonderful books. It was very nice – and the children sat around reading books and petting cats.

Walt and Matthew went fishing Sunday – some guys from Walt’s office rented a fishing boat and captain for the day – Matthew caught a shark! We learned later that there are lots of sharks around and that’s the kind of fish in the fish and chips chops. Matthew found out that he’s not wild about fishing – but did enjoy being out in Cook Straight for the day – it was a wonderful sunny and calm day. Kris Kate and I elected not to go. We stayed home and the girls baked and frosted the cake with very little supervision for me.

Tonight we go to "Wellington College – on stage" a production put on by Matt's school - he will usher.

I’ve been having trouble when running with my knees – finally had it x-rayed, which showed no abnormalities. So this A.M. I went to a sports Doctor who said I have stretched a ligament and that my knee – cap is slipping – now I go to the physical therapist for 10 treatments, take anti-inflammatory pills for 10 days, rub a medicated cream on my knee and then will have to have special inserts in my shoes – and it’s all for free – can you believe it? All personal injuries are covered under the Accident Compensation Corporation. I sure have missed running these last three weeks and will be glad to start again soon.

alt and I go to take our written driving test next week – God I hate test! (Guess I'd better not go back to school) and already have sweaty palms just thinking about it.

Thanks for the pictures – one of these days we’ll have more. I did remember Matthew‘s class picture. Matt hates this picture – says he looks fat – he’s not. Kate and Kris won’t have their’s taken until October.

Love

Pat.

Letter's Context

2026-10-30

Surprising how cold 90 %+ humidity air at 32 - 34 degrees can feel. Sucks the warmth out of your bones. The rewards were the snow capped Rimutakas  visible from our kitchen window. On adventuresome days, we could walk up to the top of Karori valley and see snow capped Southern Alps on the South Island, across Cook Strait. 


New Zealand government actively encouraged vigorous activities. Likely this reflected the Public Health System's recognition that a vigorous public was a healthier (and cheaper to keep healthy) public. Sprains aches and breaks were the flip side of a more vigorous public.

Besides Molly's meal we got her great affection for Mont-Saint-Michel and Chartres (Penguin Classics) by Henry Adams and Ray Carney  A demanding, but rewarding read.


The fishing craft was built during WW II. Wartime shortages meant the hull was mostly cement reinforced by steel. It lookes stout, rust bleeding through the concrete and somewhat ungainly. Fifteen to twenty Databank programmers and analysts crowded the deck. Tryking to cast a line midst the crowd, I succeeded in hitting Chris Studt on the head with a lead sinker. My limited enthusiasm for fishing quickly waned.

wrote July 1986 of cuteness, winter cold, fire-lighting and USA visit schedule.  —Za15

15 Lynmouth Ave.
Karori, Wellington, New Zealand

Georg Buchner 4,
6236 Eschborn 2
W Germany

For the past six months, everything has been insufferably cute. Kate and Kris have discovered the world is cute and they must be sure we are aware. Toffee the cat is sooooooooooo cute“; the Australian‘ possum perilously scaling our deck to leave his scat is cuuuuuuuuuuute. The hedgehog waddling up the back garden is soooooooooo cuuuuuuuuuute. Matthew quickly had enough of the cute world and demands they shut up whatever he thinks Pat or I are not in hearing distance. Doting parents take longer to catch on.

Last week was one of those when Pat and I had been married too long. The fire was hard to light because I bought green wood. With care and nurturing I could get a fire going, which made enough heat to dry the wood it burned. For face saving I could point to it: “there it’s not so hard to get a fire going just follow my example“.

After several failures on the cold evenings when I was late coming home, Pat‘s patience wore thin, “I can’t light the damn thing, you light it; we’re all freezing“ it was the wrong time to say, “If you just started it like I showed you“.

After several failures by the fire master, we were snapping at each other with all the practice of a quarter century of marriage. (Without a fire it was the next best way to generate a little heat.) Usually the kids get really upset when the parents quarrel, they’re afraid I’ll move all of us to Madagascar out of spite. This time Kate just gushingly observed “Daaaa-daaaaas cuuuuuute little way of making a fire and maaaaaaw-meeereeees cuuuuuuute little way of making a fire“

That stopped the quarrel immediately; we were both rendered nauseous by overdose of saccharin and took to our bed.

We plan to holiday in the states. Current plans are to leave Wellington on 12 December and stop in Los Angeles for a couple of days. The kids want to see Disneyland and so does Pat since I and every third New Zealand have gone, it’s hard to say no. We’ll fly from LA to DFW on the 14th (maybe the 15th) we plan to visit in the Fort Worth area from the 15th till the 26th. The kids insist on seeing their friends in Austin, so we’ll probably visit in Austin the 27th through the 31st December returning to Fort Worth and fly to Milwaukee on 3 January. Then we fly from Milwaukee back to Wellington on the ninth.

wrote Aug. 19, 1986 of Matthew, Kate and Kris schooling, sports activities, Pat's acceptance at Law School, . +Context

15 Lynmouth Ave..
Karori, Wellington 5, New Zealand

2114 Tierney Ave.
Ft Worth, TX

Dear Eve,

Well here it is the 19th already and I still haven’t finished (or started) this letter. The kids get out of school for two weeks at the end of this week – term break. It seems when there are school breaks, there are special movies downtown and in the suburbs for the kids; also TV programs during the day for them. Matthew, who just received his five MAD Magazines he ordered a while back, is all set – he gets three weeks and will probably be Alfred Newmaned out by that time. Did I tell you he wants to go into hotel management? Don’t know if he’s serious or not, but has been talking about it for a while now – especially the part about being trained in Switzerland!

I met with Kate & Kris’s teachers for their term reports. They are doing very well now and are just one of the kids (they do have some very nice friends here.) Kate’s teacher thinks that Kate is a real artist – and we were pleased with the way both of them express themselves on paper – lot of emphasis here on writing. Math is not one of their best subject – they grasp ideas quickly, but don’t like to spend any time Learning their tables – etc. – Would rather socialize. They love playing netball (basketball Without the dribbling) and are on the team for their classes.

Walt plays on the volleyball team Databank has. Matthew’s on his schools soccer team – I’m not on any team, but have begun running again – got new shoes – the old ones were worn out and were causing problems with my knees. But now all is well although since the shoes cost a large fortune, I hate to wear them outside. Maybe I’ll just run around inside the house!

I have decided to go back to school and would you believe I’ve been accepted in the law school at Victoria University (which is closer to us than UT was) on a part-time basis. I can take as long as I want to finish – perhaps I work a year or two before I retire Walt is being very supportive. Can’t wait for me to support him! I will start off with one course and then take two second term then see how it goes from there. The people at the university have been very friendly and helpful – And I am beginning to think I can do it. Surely I’m smarter than some of those lawyers. I’d like to go into family law anyway. I’m excited and feel satisfied having made a decision as to what I’m going to do when I grow up.

I looked at anniversary cards for you and Mike, but couldn’t find none [sic] I felt suited the occasion. So we all here in Wellington wish you two a happy anniversary.

We got a letter from Julie – she didn’t include her address. I’ll tell everyone hello - and let us know how you all are doing.

Love

Pat

Kate & Kris talk about writing Amy – but haven’t in a while. They always feel so proud to get a letter from Amy – I’ll try to get them to write next week. They were really good about, writing it first but have gradually gotten worse.

One of these days we will have some pictures to send – just have a few left on the role.

Bought a little donkey tail plant the other day – also a Portulacara Afra (small Jade). Passed our driving test –

Letter's Context

2026-10-30

I had had a heart attack earlier that year (I believe it was that year) so the possibility that Pat could become a single Mom was not lost on us. Preparing to have an adequate income was a responsible preparation for possible future difficulties.

Moving to a different country, to a different job, to a different economy and different social mores meant unanticipated stresses. A few work colleagues were needlessly stressful.

My experience makes me more sympathetic to immigrants. To any country.

For a while after the attack, I stopped commuting to work by bicycle and took the bus. Eventually angina abated and I resumed bicycling

Dear Eve

Here it is birthday time again I just finished baking Walt a chocolate marble poundcake with chocolate glaze. I can’t wait to taste it. It looks wonderful. Matthew wants a cherry pie instead of a cake. I just have to find the cherries. He also wants chicken fried steak for his birthday dinner and a shirt, tie and slacks for his present -what happened to that little boy who wanted Star Wars figures and laser guns! They both get the cards you sent - thank you. Where in New Zealand are those people you met from and why did they move to US? Just curious

I have received my official admission to Law School. It begins next March and goes through October. That’s the university academic year. I wish I could start now - I’m ready.

Peg and I went to visit the gardens of the American & Swiss ambassador’s house on Sunday. We also visited Vogel house Gardens (that’s the home of the NZ Prime Minister). They (the Gardens) were beautiful - the huge tulip trees like we saw in Michigan were in their glory as well as azalea rhododendron‘s, - and lots of other spring flowers. We stopped for tea and scones at the Anglican church - all in all it was a very pleasant outing

We haven’t done much else, but since it seems spring has arrived - and the days are warm and sunny, we hope to see more of the countryside. During the winter. We found a small ice cream place at a beach - (Days Bay) - Do you ever read Katherine Mansfield? She’s a New Zealand writer - lived here in Karori for a while , and some of her descriptions of Wellington are still very good (accurate) after 50 years). Anyway we hope to get back to the blue penguin ice cream parlor and take a walk and that beautiful beach

(The ice cream here in New Zealand is not very good compared to Bluebell and Swenson’s - but the blue Penguin’s compares quite well. - I love to get the coffee ice cream dipped in chocolate. (I noticed that I’ve written a lot about food. - I think it’s because for three weeks I have not eaten any between meal snacks - doing OK but sure do think about eats a lot.)

My knee is OK so I’ve been running again. It seems that I stretched a ligament because my shoes were worn out. But with my new shoes I just sail along well not quite sail but at least I can run without pain.

We are going to have a party - since we’ve been here a year - and have invited about 20 people. I hope it will be fun at least not a disaster. A lot of people we’ve met through Data Bank are from other countries - so we will have people here from the US (Peg and Hugh Gilman), New Zealand, England, Canada, Argentina, Finland, and Portugal. Sounds so international doesn’t it. And Switzerland Forgot about the Bopps, These people for the most part don’t know each other - just us - I’m not cooking (here. I go with food again) but will have ham, corn beef lots of bread and veggies for sandwiches. Wish you could come!

Mary and Jimmy are still planning on coming at Christmas. (Do I tell you things over and over? I’m sorry if I do)

I’ve got to stop now must go to veggie market and post office and pick up Kris & Kate.

Love

Pat

wrote Sep. 29, 1986 of special return home, remu wall unit, Pat, Kate, Kris, Matthew and Pohutakawa bowl —Za5

15 Lynmouth Ave.
Karori, Wellington, New Zealand

Georg Buchner 4,
6236 Eschborn 2
W Germany

Ordinarily, my coming home is relatively quiet. I take a bicycle, keep the gate from slamming, and quietly show myself in. Today, quite unusual, Kate and Kris greeted me at the door. The Christmas box had arrived from Germany and Pat decreed we should all be present when it was opened. I savored their anticipation, prolonging my winding down. Hang up my clothes, bag, back outside to hang up my wet raincoat in the garage, into my dressing area to wipe the rain from my glasses. Kris, eager to hasten the process bent down to remove my fluorescent leg bands. Then we greedily tore into the box.

29 September 1986.

Some letters wait around a bit. This one got postponed for a miscellaneous reasons. One was to put up a wall unit in the snug. Like most undertakings, it took far longer than expected. The TV and amp were on a table, and it was getting quite messy. So the wall unit. I used native remu, which can be quite beautiful; some pieces are highly variable in color, ranging from cream to dark brown areas. It can also be soft and a little splintery. Remu takes 200 years to mature so timber companies would much rather clear out the remu and plant pine, which will crop seven or eight times in that span.

The biggest thing going on now is GST: goods and service tax. It’s rather like sales tax. But it starts at a whopping 10%. Income taxes will be reduced accordingly so supposedly my tax burden should be unchanged. But the country seems to be in the grips of a buying frenzy. Everyone wants to beat GST, that is make ones certain purchases before the added 10% is applied. I didn’t realize how pervasive this idea was until the time I went outside in the rain to try to diagnose a window leak. I was on a ladder, with only my white gum boots visible through the window. Kate, inside looked up with alarm and asked her mother who was outside. Pat said Santa Klaus. Kate caught on and retorted, “oh, he’s trying to beat GST.“

Matthew got a book on paper airplanes for his birthday. Airplanes with a Klein – Fogelman notch wing. We spent a few hours making airplanes and flying them. He also got a shirt, tie, and pants for wearing to dances. Thanks to his grandmother. He looked quite nice in his shirt and ties; he has to remind me; he’s 14. Kris still deposits food in her room. In her school pack, her treasure chest, and probably in place not yet uncovered. She’s not at all dim, but she must think her parents so.

Pat got me a Pohutakawa  bowl for my birthday. She saw me admiring it in the gallery. It’s a lovely chocolate brown colored wood not at all practical for utility though, because the wood can be so unstable. It’s really strictly for appearance and must be guarded against water and sunlight. The form is seemingly robust, the surface very smooth, but the artisan deliberately left signs of the woods instability, four deep irregular pits marked the surface, telling a tale of the woods, torturous growth against the smooth even curve formed on the lathe.

Love.

Walton.

wrote Oct 29, 1986 of Kate & Kris's birthday party, Matthew's paper route, Law School in March, Walt's wall unit. +Context

15 Lynmouth Ave..
Karori, Wellington 5, New Zealand

2114 Tierney Ave.
Ft Worth, TX

Dear Eve,

Spring is here finally – well actually it’s been around a while in the form of rain – rain and more rain. This past weekend was sunny after at least four that were not. Kate & Kris had their birthday on a rainy Saturday; 14 little girls came and giggled, squealed, and had a good time at the first (and last) birthday party in New Zealand. (It rained on their birthday last year too, but they didn’t have a party.) Parties here are a little different. Everyone comes for a meal – of pizza, hotdogs, sausage, rolls, etc. – anything that can be construed as junk food and then the birthday girl’s (in this case girls’) parents pile everyone in their car to take them home. After three hours, that was the best time for me and Walt. The party was enlivened by a gift of a Whoopie cushion which sent these giggly girls into even greater gales. We thought we had bought tons of food – but it disappeared in minutes ( the only thing green on the table was grapes – so nobody could say, yuck). Fortunately after two games which involved wholesale eating of chocolate, everyone did get their fill. Kate & Kris said they had a wonderful time – and understand that we can’t do that every year – I hope.

Matthew has a paper route (paper round it’s called here). The little girl who delivered our paper left for France. (her mother will study cello in Paris) for five months and wanted someone to take her place while she’s gone. So Matthew has his first job. he delivers 42 papers on our street, and the two below. It only takes him about 30 minutes – and it’s just six days a week – and he will earn $22.50 a fortnight (every two weeks). He’s quite pleased – even though it has rained on him just about every day so far – and is busy, figuring out ways to spend his salary. Thanks for that lovely picture of Amy. Can’t believe she’s a senior this year. What are her plans for college?

Kate and Kris will have their pictures taken at school tomorrow – I don’t know how long it takes to get them – maybe with Christmas cards you’ll get them. Walt had a roll of film developed, but the results were not too good. We take more soon.

Can’t think of much else going on. I will be going to Law School beginning in March. I know it will take a long time, but I’m going to try anyway. I don’t just want to go out and type for someone; I want someone to type for me! Did I tell you that Victoria University is quite close to us – closer than UT was to us in Austin. I could just hop on the bus or drive, but parking is a problem) and be there in a few minutes. I’m really looking forward to going – and wish I didn’t have to wait until March (the academic year is March – November) Walt built a beautiful wall unit for the TV, stereo, etc. It’s made out of native remu (or rimu ) and it really makes the room look neat. Also he’s in the process of making bookshelves for my study, which is also the computer room, music, practice room, etc.).

Must close, the bread is ready to come out of the oven – and I have to work in school library this afternoon.

All hello to all

Love Pat

The wall unit (7' x 9' x 14") had to e disassembled for transport (New Zealand to Oregon to Austin to Austin to Knoxville) To be more practical, it became 3 bookshelves.

wrote Dec 11, 1986 of Thanksgiving with the Gilmans, Mary and Jimmy‘s visit, Matt, Kate, Kris, zebra finches and Pat's knee.

15 Lynmouth Ave..
Karori, Wellington 5, New Zealand

2114 Tierney Ave.
Ft Worth, TX

Dear Eve,

Did there used to be so little time between Thanksgiving and Christmas? Time seems to go faster and faster, doesn’t it? And the old skin sags more and more (at least mine does).

We did celebrate Thanksgiving this year – with the Gilman‘s. They immigrated here from Boston. Hugh works at databank, they have three boys 15, 13 and 10. Peg‘s mother just moved over here too – so we all had Thanksgiving with all the trimmings – even pecan pie. (I did pay a fortune for those nuts.) Margaret, Peg mom bought and cooked the turkey, Peg did all the veggies and the pumpkin pie. I did homemade rolls fruit salad and the pie. It really was just like Thanksgiving – we had a good time.

That was two Sundays ago. This Sunday we go to the annual Christmas picnic where Father Christmas makes an appearance. we are all looking forward to Mary and Jimmy‘s visit. They will spend a week at the condo at Lake Taupo – about 200 km (200 miles) from here. There will be enough room for us to join them for that week (Christmas) and then we’ll all come back here For another week. We hope to take the boat across to the South Island and spend some time in the Marlboro sounds – that depends on the weather.

Today is the last day of school for Matthew. I think he’s had a good year – and enjoyed his classmates – for the most part. he still gets teased a bit about being an American, but if he weren’t, I’m sure he would get teased about being short, having freckles or just breathing. Boys are 13–14 (I don’t know about other ages) are really hard on each other. He has done well at school, but could’ve done better – he says now. (he will forget that until the end of next year).

Kate and Kris have another week to go. They even sound like the kids at school now. They’ve made a lot of friends – and still talk all the time. Kate and Kris and Matt saveed their money and bought two zebra finches - named Austin and Carrot. Maybe with vacation they will take some time to write Amy - they miss hearing from her.

My knee is all better so I have I have been running every morning – I walk the girls to school (18 minutes) run back home through two parks and several side streets (30 minutes), climb back up The hill (72 steps) and walk to the house (10 minutes). So I get an hour of exercise about five days a week. My friend Pat has been finishing up her masters degree and will start running again next week. I look forward to that, it’s hard to make myself go by myself.

We do have some pictures to send, but there was a special on five by sevens, so now I need to get some large envelopes. Of course, there’s not one picture that’s good of all of us – I may send you several.

We hope you have a new nice Christmas – and do write.

Love

Pat.

wrote Jan. 2, 1987 of Xmas $ from Hazel's widower, VCR purchase, copy Monty Python, tool thieft. . +Context

Eve Fox
2114 Tierney Ave
Ft Worth, TX

15 Lynmouth Ave
Karori, Wellington 5, New Zealand

Dear Pat,

I said I would write after Christmas, but I thought it would be sooner. I may not ever get this finished or mailed today. We received some Christmas money from Hazel‘s husband Hubert – a real shock! Anyway, we used it to buy a V, C. R.– Something we have always wanted. We rarely go anywhere so we have really been using it. We are in the process of taping all of the Monty Python episodes – channel 13 has started running them again. They began a couple of months ago, but hopefully they’ll keep them going for over and over so we can get them all. I have also checked out movies from the library (no charge) and rented once. I guess we should have paid bills, but we are enjoying it so much that I haven’t had time to feel guilty. We really enjoyed our Christmas. Julie was here, of course, and Yoichi came up from for New Year’s.

One unfortunate happening, that is costing us, money and heartache; on the night before New Year’s Eve, Mike had all of his tools stolen out of the back of his truck. In 13 years we’ve never had anything stolen. What kind of creep would steal a poor working man’s tools. They were tools. He’s collected over 25 or 30 years and were of no value except to us. He needs them to work. Now we’ll have to go charge new ones and a toolbox too. If we had the cash, it wouldn’t be too bad. I hate paying on things – it seems like forever.

I haven’t thanked you for the great seeds! We joined the Fort Worth botanical Society so we took them out there and they are going to start them for us in exchange for a sample for their new Arboretum that just opened last fall. I can hardly wait to see them growing. We might have to keep them in the greenhouse year-round – we’ll have to see how your things grow here. My corn plant bloomed. I didn’t know they did. I am going to donate it to one of the new zoo buildings for their offices. It is so tall I won’t be able to winter it another year. I hope to be able to send you some pictures soon, when Amy uses up the rest in her camera.

I know you have enjoyed your sister and brother-in-law‘s visit. I have got some good skin care products for you if I can figure out a way to get them there without breaking only one is in a plastic container. They are all great for “dry, over 40 skin“ like ours.

I am sure there is something else I want to say but just can’t think of it here it is “night“ already and I still haven’t finished writing this and still can’t think of what it was. I wanted to say. I guess it could wait until next time.

Love and write soon

Eve

P. S. We have really missed Hazel the past couple of weeks. I saw something recently that she would think funny and I could almost hear her laugh. As long as our parents “the ones left,“ are alive. I never want to be far away. Sorry, just a touch of the post holiday blues.

wrote Jan 4, 1987 of Lake Taupo, Mary & Jimmy, geothermal sites, South Island, Christmas, Matt, Kate Kris and Walt and Law School. +Context

15 Lynmouth Ave..
Karori, Wellington 5, New Zealand

2114 Tierney Ave.
Ft Worth, TX

Dear Eve,

We hope you had a nice Christmas. Did Santa Claus come? We had a visit not only from father Xmas, but also Mary & Jimmy  . We had a really good time. We met him up at Lake Taupo  (about 250 miles from Wellington) on Tuesday before Christmas. They belong to an international timeshare thingy, so we had a lovely five days with great views, indoor pool – spa, etc. – all very unlike New Zealand, but the Americans love it. We saw lots of geysers, bubbling, mud, pits, Volcanic lakes, waterfalls, and lots of native woods (or bush as it’s called here). We walked a lot, swam, ate turkey for dinner (also pecan pie) and talked. When we headed back to Wellington for another week of sightseeing, we took them to our favorite places, saw a New Zealand movie cartoon ate at a wonderful restaurant, made homemade ice cream – and on and on. One thing we did was to take the ferry across the Cook Strait  to see the South Island  . It was a three and one a half hour trip there and the scenery was wonderful – the Marlborough Sounds  are quite spectacular. And it was a beautiful sunny day. Except for a day or two the weather was great the whole time Mary & Jimmy were here (I don’t think they realized how lucky they were – it is very early summer and the weather can be quite cold wet– but it wasn’t too bad at all. One day was very, very windy.)

Now they are gone and we are getting back to normal.

For Christmas. Walt gave me a beautiful wooden bowl. It was made by an Auckland artist out of native Totara. Now I need to find a good place to show it off. (Did you get the seeds we sent? I hope they germinate.) Matthew Kate & Kris at the age were clothes delight them so that’s what they got. Kate & Kris squealed with delight when they saw their much coveted Adidas track pants under the tree, they were still not “into“ dresses, but have definite opinions about what kind of sweatshirts they should have! Walt got a sweater from me a New Zealand wool of course. Mary & Jimmy bought us a suitcase full of things, vanilla, Pace Picante Sauce, Reese‘s Peanut Butter Cups, pencil, sharpeners, and pencils that actually have erasers on the top! It was a real treasure trove.

Did you have Julie home for the holidays?

The kids don’t go back to school until 1 February and I don’t go until 1 March. I’m already ready to go. Pat Tyler the lady I run with, has a friend who just finished her first year at law school, and Pat is going to have us both over for coffee to talk about what I can expect. The lady, Chris, also said she would bring her papers from her classes so that I could read them. I thought that was awfully nice – and since I’ve been out of school for ever will be a big help.

We are all doing well– and hope you are too. Please write soon

Love

Pat

PS I ran over 550 miles last year – but not all at once.

Letter's Context

2026-10-31

Jimmy had asked what we needed from the United States; before the internet, much was unobtainable, like repair parts for Sears devices, Pace brand Pecante Sauce, etc.

At 123 Campbell, our neighbors were the Tylers, Pat and Bryan. Brian had been educated at Harvard and was the Auditor General of New Zealand. Pat and Pat got along very well. New Zealanders were much more active than Americans. Many at my work had dreams of Olympic glory. Others had family trampolines out front, available to any neighborhood children. Fear of lawsuits over injured children didn't exist; National Health Care took care of any injuries. And there were many sports injuries.

wrote Jan. 11, 1987 of Xmas pkg. Prairie Home Companion, Steve Bottorff and girls pictures. +Context

15 Lynmouth Ave..
Karori, Wellington 5, New Zealand

2114 Tierney Ave.
Ft Worth, TX

We got the package yesterday. It’s too late for the Xmas decorations – anyway, we didn’t decorate a tree this year (took a small one to Lake Taupo which we decorated with Xmas crackers  . (I’ll bring you some right next Xtmas)) but the kids did like them. And what a wonderful book I’m on page 143 – and read Walt the good parts (I love the story of Leon and Roman). We miss hearing “Prairie Home Companion“. We were semi – regulars listeners. We always wish they were on at a different time. (I think I was it was 9 o’clock on Saturday nights. In Fort Worth. It’s around six isn’t it?) Anyway it’s really nice to read the book– ( I love Garrison‘s voice)

It’s been hot and sunny and dry the past few weeks (only 77°F, but somehow it’s hot here.)

Got a letter from an old friend (from Ann Arbor) [Steve Bottorff] who is getting married again January 31 and then he and his wife are moving to Japan. Wonder if they’ll drop in since we share the same ocean. his son who was in fourth grade when we knew them, has now graduated from college and is a reporter in DC. Where does the time go? What are your and Mike’s plans once Amy has left? We have years left. Probably seven or eight at least. I know once it’s gone, it will have seemed to be quick but on days when all three are teasing each other it seems eternities away.

Must go now – Hope you enjoy the pictures.

Love

Pat

At K & K’s school individual pictures are taken every other year – so you’ll have to wait for those.

Letter's Context

2026-10-30

Both Pat and Steve Bottorff had worked at Parke-Davis Laboratory in Ann Arbor, we had exchanged many social visits.

wrote Feb. 19, 1987 of birthdays, Louise, Bob, Garrison Keeler, Kate, Kris, Matt, mail and cyst removal..

15 Lynmouth Ave..
Karori, Wellington 5, New Zealand

2114 Tierney Ave.
Ft Worth, TX

Dear Eve,

Finally, it has rained. It was quite dry here – fire bans, on barbecues, rubbish, burning, etc. Sometimes it does get dry toward March and the fire danger is great because of the wind, but this year started out dry. Anyway, we had a lot of hot, dry weather – enough so that I was glad to see it end.

Thank you for the birthday card; hope you got the one we sent you. Did you have a nice birthday. I did. Walt’s mother called (I can remember when that wouldn’t have seems so special) and so did Walt’s brother. Walt gave me a beautiful leather satchel – I think he’s trying to tell me something! – For school. Unfortunately another year came with the day too!

I finished reading Lake Woebegone Days and enjoyed it – it was great fun wasn’t it? I’ve loaned it to a friend mother [Margaret Huntington, mother of Peg Gilman] (American) who misses hearing the program on Saturday nights – she’s even been to the broadcast – says you need to get tickets four months in advance.

The kids are back in school have been since the first of this month. Kris was home today because of an earache. Kate missed eating lunch with Kris, but at least she went on to school without a fuss. I remember in the past, having to promise, the one who is not sick the moon practically before she would go on to school. Music lessons begin this Saturday. Kate still plays the cello; Kris the violin. Matthew is taking French horn lessons at his school – and is supposed to be checking on how to get in the orchestra, but unless someone comes up to him and gives him an engraved invitation, he probably won’t be in it. What a kid!

We liked hearing about the seeds – what a good idea to take them to the botanical gardens. If you need some more, let me know. Walt keeps putting in more trees here. Recently, he added a tulip tree and a Babaco. We’ve played 50 (small) trees and so far more have died – Well one did but the nursery replaced it. Can you believe the owner of the nursery came out to the house to check the tree – wanted to see why it died – it was an Australian wattle tree, which was replaced – but the replacement is not too happy either – must be the location.

I think when it finally expires, we put in a native palm tree. It would look good in front of the white wall. Are you getting excited about the wedding? Wish we could come.

By the way – when you send a package, you can count it taking two months, unless it’s airmail which cost a fortune. The deadline for mailing packages to arrive at Xmas time is something like October 20. I don’t know what “first class“ means just tell him you want to send it “surface” – that is if you mail me again. is Amy getting excited about graduation? What will you do when she leaves? When it seems strange – maybe a few more cats? I love that article you sent. Some days I can’t wait

Hope to hear from you soon

Love

Pat

PS I had a small cyst removed from my face last week – don’t know if you’ll notice it but I’m so glad to have it gone the MD said she wouldn’t even bother to have a biopsy done since it was obviously just a fatty deposit.

wrote Mar 26, 1987 of enjoying classes, high failure rate, Matthew, Kate and Kris baking for school fairs, Julie's wedding.

15 Lynmouth Ave..
Karori, Wellington 5, New Zealand

2114 Tierney Ave.
Ft Worth, TX

Dear Eve,

It seems funny that you’re getting spring fever and we are beginning Autumn ( not fall here). It seems even funnier that here I am back in school. It’s been three weeks now and I’m really enjoying my classes – just wish I were smarter. I just me [sic] one class this term, but I have two lectures and one tutorial. The tutorial is a small class – just 12 of us – and I’m old enough to be not only the mother of all the students, but the lecturer as well.) The lectures, Legal Systems are quite large – probably 75–100 students, yet we have a seating chart and we are all called on for answers. I’m enjoying this course more than any I’ve ever had – but it’s lots of work ( Our first assignment is due Monday – we are given a legal problem to solve taking all three sides – the defense, prosecution and judicial ) and 75% of the students taking this course fail. I will try not to be in that group, but sometimes I just know I will be. Oh for some confidence!

Matthew’s school is having a fair on Saturday and strangely enough, it’s Walt who has to work in a booth. Matthew will bake a cake for the bake sale. Kate & Kris will bake cookies. I’m really enjoying the kids (more or maybe not) now that they are getting older and really participating - like doing the dishes and the cooking! I do remind them to write, but I guess as they get more involved here, the less time they spend writing to the states. Good & bad sides to that, isn’t there ?

How are the wedding plans going? Are you going to pull down the old garage completely? What will you use for storage then? Sorry to make this short, but I have to leave now, but I wanted you to know that I’m thinking about you - and enjoy getting your letters.

We do get Time Magazine here (for me and Matt - Walt hates Time, but the Newsweek is included in the business magazine from Australia and is too expensive). And so did see an article that ole Garrison was moving to Sweden (or Denmark). Also, we keep up with the downfall of the Reagans (which we love)

Write soon

Love Pat

wrote May 19, 1987 of wedding, school break, study difficult, cultural stew, sleeping lofts and garden. +Context

15 Lynmouth Ave..
Karori, Wellington 5, New Zealand

2114 Tierney Ave.
Ft Worth, TX

Dear Eve,

How was the wedding? Did it rain? Will the newlyweds continue to live on Tierney? We got Amy’s announcement - and her lovely picture. We are in the middle of May break – the kids (and I) are off from school for two weeks. It’s nice here they have two weeks off often – and the most at one time is six weeks – at Xmas. However, I think that will change soon since people feel that six weeks is just too long. Actually, two weeks is great because they don’t have time to get real bored. Kate & Kris and Matthew have been to several movies, had friends over, been over to friends, had lunch with Walt, etc. And I’m ready for them to go back next week. I’m supposed to be studying for a test this next Tuesday – and it’s hard with everyone around all the time.

Walt and I are going to a Cambodian festival Saturday – there are lots of Asians in New Zealand – matter of fact, there are lots of people from everywhere – especially Asian & Great Britain & Europe. Not a lot from US but quite a few from Canada, I’m surprised that New Zealand is so multi - cultural, maybe it’s just Wellington – because it’s the capital – there are areas of Greeks, Indians, Dutch Landers, Finnish, etc., and just about every area of New Zealand. And since Chernobyl there has been an increased interest in immigration from Sweden.

I read in time magazine about the fall of Gary Hart. Did anyone in your house? Feel sad?

Walt built a loft in Kate‘s room. The closet had a flat area above it since the ceiling is one of those A-frame styles. So he enlarged it, put in a railing and quicker than he got it built, Kris moved out of her room and into Kate’s. They really like it – (and Matthew wants one now.) They keep all their stuffed animals up there, sleep there – and curl up with a book up there. The only other exciting thing is that we bought and planted a lovely Japanese maple. There are few deciduous trees here in New Zealand I felt we needed something to remind us that it really is autumn in May. Hope it does well. How are the New Zealand plants doing? Do you need more?

Love, Pat.

Letter's Context

2026-10-31

No longer sure why i thought the sleeping loft useful. Perhaps the girls bed rooms had too little space for their activities. The viewing angle of the Karori valley from the loft was superb.

For the mattress, I bought a futon; heavy, stuffed with cotton. I expected Kate to turn the mattress at least weekly. Was disappointed to find she had never turned it. When I tried to turn the futon in the cramped, confined space of the loft, it was nearly imossible for me. Poor planning on my part.

wrote June 26, 1987 of earrings, law school, 60% attrrition, Walt, fruit, meals on wheels. +Context

15 Lynmouth Ave..
Karori, Wellington 5, New Zealand

2114 Tierney Ave.
Ft Worth, TX

Dear Eve,

Am glad Amy liked the earrings? Did you? Would you like a pair? I think they are quite pretty, but since I don’t wear earrings, don’t know if they are too large and clunky, or what. They do look lovely on the lady who sells them.

I am in the middle of a two week study break. The classes which go for just 1/2 the year are having finals now. My classes are full year ones so no finals for me at this stage. I am starting another class though next week. I’m looking forward to it – and wished I had taken more at the first of the year. I will take a full load next year – if I pass. There are 500 of us trying for 200 places in the second year. And the competition is heating up. I’m holding my own - wish I were doing better but. I’ve done well on all my assignments, but the first test I failed! Did quite well on the next test though. But the thing that really scares me is that the final exam is 100% of the grade. I have to make 100 points through the year on assignments and tests out of 200 to be able to set the final exam. So far I’m OK – but just the fact that it all rides on one test is so frightening.

The kids liked seeing that Garfield comic strip and Matthew wondered if you could send the whole comic page sometime.

It has been cold and wintery here – but today the sun is out.

We’ve all been doing OK – Walt has been working quite a bit of overtime lately. We will all be glad when the special project is out of the way.

Matthew finally joined the orchestra at his school. Kate & Kris continue to take her music lessons on Saturday mornings. We are going to a Fourth of July party at our American friends house. She’s making hamburgers and I’m bringing the apple pie. Have we had our fill of apples lately. Walt bought a 36 pound bag (no I guess 40 pound bags -18 kilos each) of granny Smith apples for $14 (and they threw in 5 kg of kiwi fruit – which I have acquired an insatiable taste for). So we’ve had apple pies, apple cakes, apple, jam, apples, etc. Etc. Etc. We’ve gotten through one bag and have started on the second. I have to go now - started driving for meals on wheels on Fridays – and must leave now –

Love

Pat

PS sorry didn’t realize it was so late - hello to all.

Letter's Context

2026-10-30

We tried to save on groceries by shopping the producers' markets. New Zealand is an exporter of food, so export commodities were often over produced. Thus 10 or even 20 kilos of frozen flounder or orange roughy could be a good buy. Ditto Apples, Kiwi fruit and lamb.

wrote Sep 1, 1987 of earrings, school breaks, Evelyn's help, Amy, Julie, U S trip itinerary, dinner guests, red rock seals.

15 Lynmouth Ave..
Karori, Wellington 5, New Zealand

2114 Tierney Ave.
Ft Worth, TX

Dear Eve,

Have you got the earrings and like them. The little shop where I buy them – and other neat crafty things – is moving from Karori Mall to the other side of town. I’ll miss it. A few weeks ago I bought a beautiful Indian silk scarf (really a shawl) with long red fringe. Maybe it’s good the shop moved – now I can afford food for the table.

We are all doing fine. We are on break – and usually it’s cold and rainy at this time – but it’s like summer. In fact, this whole winter has been a lot like summer. We had one light frost – and no southerly gale force winds. Anyway, all four of us are out of school for two weeks. Unfortunately, my two weeks don’t coincide with theirs – had one week without them – and they will have one without me. It works out OK except that they on Tuesdays and Thursdays when I’m gone all day. My neighbor Evelyn has offered to have them all over for lunch on those days. She has no kids and loves cooking lunch for them. Evelyn, from the Philippines, (is married to an American, who is over here for one year) was a TV star in the Philippines, but left when Marcos took over the TV channel. She wrote comedy shows, act in them, hosted game shows, talkshows, etc. She’s only 31 now so she must’ve been a child prodigy but she is cute and fun to be with.

Is Amy already for college? What is Julie up to these days? Where is she staying in Austin or what? Have you been there lately?

Our plans for our visit are coming along. We leave here on 12 December – Spend two days in Disneyland, arrive in FW on the 16th. We’ll stay there until the 12th, then go to Austin for a week (the kids would never forgive us if they couldn’t see their old friends), go back to Fort Worth the second. On 3 January, we will fly up to Wisconsin, fly back to New Zealand on the ninth. We are looking forward to it.

Walt and I went to see “Crimes of the Heart“ last night. I really enjoyed it – did you see it? We don’t go to very many movies – and the ones I usually insist on seeing are what Walt calls my women movies – The Color Purple, Heartburn, Out of Africa. I told him he doesn’t have to come, but he does – and he did enjoy Crimes of the Heart much more than any of the others.

We are having a few friends over for dinner – a guy who is in my law classes and a woman plus her husband. I will feed them red beans and tacos. Hope they like them. Walt and I ate at the Mexican Cantina a couple of weeks ago – and it was surprisingly good – but expensive! However, they make their own tortillas which they sell in packages – those those with the Pace’s picante sauce my sister brought over should make the tacos tasty.

Sunday we went out to see the seals at Red Rock. We had gone out before, but it was so windy, we had to turn around that time. The wind was so strong Kate and Kris couldn’t walk – in fact several times they came close to being literally blown away. What a strange experience that was. This time, however there was or no wind and we had a lovely hours walk to see those animals sunning themselves. The smell was awful!

It was so nice to be out of school for a while – no studying. But I’m beginning to miss it. If I pass this year, I plan to take a full load next year. If I fail, I don’t know what I’ll do! Oh well I don’t have to face that yet. I took my terms test last week and I think I at least passed. Must go – got to take the kids out for a while.

Love

Pat

Letter's Context

2026-09-28


It’s a rather English/Kiwi case of taking the piss out of Daddy; the family are becoming kiwi acculturated. “Taking the piss” is a Commonwealth idiom for deflating false pride. Hey, it’s more like a futile contest against reality, fighting one’s age.

In my Databank job setting, my age (47) was a liability. Most of my coworkers were 10 to 25 years younger The age difference did not help. Perhaps more accurately, I found few ways to use it to my advantage.

wrote Oct. 20, 1987 of birthdays and law school final exam, lunch with friend, Louise's heart attack Walt, Matt, Kate and Kris. +Context

15 Lynmouth Ave..
Karori, Wellington 5, New Zealand

2114 Tierney Ave.
Ft Worth, TX

Dear Eve,

Thanks for remembering birthdays – and I’m sorry that I forgot. But tell Amy and Julie happy birthday from us. Some year I’ll surprise you and remember everyone’s. Birthdays this year were really low-key in this house. Matthew didn’t want to “do“ anything – just to have his palm crossed with silver. He quit his paper round after a year – to take karate two days a week – and misses the money. Walt just wanted a subscription to the Economist. Kate and Kris unfortunately had their birthdays the day before my big important final. Walt cooked lasagna for them and made the cake while I studied, and I studied all day and the whole week before. The exam counts 100% of the course class grade – three hours long – and so much to do that there was little time to think. I came out not knowing if I passed or not, and won’t know until around the first of December. Most of the people sitting the exam also felt the same insecure way I do – we’ve been ringing each other with consolations and self derogatories hoping we scraped past – to go on to do another year. Don’t know if it’s worth it or not. I have one more final next week – also three hours long – and I’m not looking forward to it, but it can’t be as bad as yesterday. How are the things going at UTA. Does Amy like her courses?

I went out to lunch today with a friend. I met when we first moved here. She had just moved here from England – next week she is moving to Auckland, where her husband will be the manager of a yuppie style hotel in the middle of a vineyard – supposedly in the style of informal yet elegant French hotels – where one spends hours eating and drinking. Heinz is a Swiss trained hotel manager, and was sought after for this position. He, Sandy and the two boys will live in a rose covered (literally) cottage in the Vineyard. Sounds idyllic doesn’t it. Anyway, so Sandy and I had lunch at the Mexican Cantina – not the Mexican Inn, but still a tasty place. We had tacos that were quite good except I don’t think they had been near jalapeño. But it was better than the taco I had once that was filled with cottage cheese and pineapple (Polynesian style = pineapple)

Walt’s Mother, sister, and niece were due to arrive here next Tuesday, but Louise had a heart attack about three weeks ago – so I guess instead of being in Germany at Christmas they will still be in Fort Worth.

Matthew got stung by a bee on Saturday and he turned into Bigfoot and has been out of school for two days cause he can’t get his shoe on. The swelling is going down now.

Kate is sitting across from me doing a report on Mexico and it’s getting hard to concentrate on this letter – she is either asking me questions or telling me something, she’s discovered about the country. Kris is up in her room with the binoculars spying on the neighbors. I hope she tells me if she sees something interesting.

I can’t wait till next week – I’m going to read a book that has no redeeming value at all – don’t know what it will be but I’m looking forward to it. But also, we I have to clean this house– it even looks dirty without my glasses.

Can’t think of anything interesting this happened around here lately. Thought Amy might enjoy the article – they still can’t get Humphrey [Elephant seal attracted to cows] (his new name) to leave the farm. They filled him full of tranquilizers, but no effect because of the blubber. They tried to chase (guide) him back to sea, but he wouldn’t have it – so a week later – Humphrey’s still around – flirting with the cows.

Love

Pat

Letter's Context

2026-11-01

Sometime about September 30, 1987, Walt's Mother, Louise had a heart attack. The record shows up in a letter from Pat to Eve in October of 1987. Mother's declining health also shows up in the absence of letters and the first letter breaking that lacuna with her reference to radiation burns - yet more assaults on her well being; this time breast cancer, I believe. Louise would die August 18, 1989.

One motivation for Pat's Law School work was grounded in my heart attack earlier in 1986; should my health fail drastically, Pat might need substantial income to raise 3 children. Pat and I failed to inform the children of the stakes involved in Patricia's schooling.

wrote Aug. 10, 1988 of dropping out of school, set on return to U. S., Walt rejects Texas. +Context

15 Lynmouth Ave..
Karori, Wellington 5, New Zealand

2114 Tierney Ave.
Ft Worth, TX

Dear Eve,

Don’t faint! Sorry I haven’t written in a while. Things here have been turbulent since our return from the states. I couldn’t get the classes I wanted to take; took some I didn’t want to take because everything was full! Dropped those - I am just too old to stay up late trying to figure out propositional calculus – somehow my philosophy class was more logic than I was prepared for. Anyway I dropped that and am not in school now. I miss it in a way, but it’s nice not to have all that pressure.

The big news is – – we are all ready to move back to the states – and have our house on the market at this time (in fact, someone is looking at this moment).

We haven’t decided where we will go – only that it probably won’t be Texas. (We had to make some concessions to Walt who is not ready as we are – but ready all the same). I don’t know how long it will take to sell the house (it’s been on the market for two weeks) but we have to sell it before we can go back. There are a lot of houses on the market at the present time - and ours is on the “wrong” end of Karori – which is how we could afford it in the first place. We hope to be out of here around the time school finishes here (mid – December) but who knows.

Otherwise we are all OK. When you recover, write and let me know how you guys are.

Love.

Letter's Context

2026-11-01

Reagan's reign was coming to an end, Gorbachev and Bush looked like more reasonable leaders, but I still feared that Austin was too attractive as a target. Plus, I didn't like Texas' heat.

wrote Sep. 15 , 1988 of South Island road trip, farm stay and housing market. +Context

15 Lynmouth Ave..
Karori, Wellington 5, New Zealand

2114 Tierney Ave.
Ft Worth, TX

Dear Eve,

We had a wonderful trip down to the south island. It is really a spectacular place, and I’m glad we got to see it even if it did cost a fortune. We got to the point where we didn’t even bother to look at the snow covered mountains or the beautiful sea views. Because there were just so much to see. The weather was good too – even though there were bad storms in Wellington while we were gone. We stayed for five days on a sheep farm (cheaper than motels) and the last day we got a grand tour of the place. it was lambing season so there were lots of babies around mini twins and triplets so Kate & Kris felt at home (they ooed and aaed so much at the lambs that we finally told them to stuff it). We also went to a neighboring farm where sheep were being shared. We were surprised that the sheep were so docile. Sounds boring, but I thought it was really interesting to see what goes on down on the farm. Those farmers, wife included, really work hard. I thought you just bought the things and then a year later you sheared them, but it seems that there’s always something these animals require anyway it was educational – and free.

We also took boat trips (into Milford sound), the kids skied, ice skated – and we all canoed on Avon river in Christchurch. We went to a little town that was a gold mining town years ago – and panned for gold. Had as much luck as, when we dug for diamonds in Arkansas. I will send some seeds soon – sometimes it’s hard to find them. I’m glad you enjoy your job. Too bad you can’t get paid for it. Maybe soon they’ll have an opening.

Our house is still for sale. No buyers yet, but maybe since spring is here, more people will start looking. I hope so. I get depressed thinking that we’re stuck here for good.

Matthew has been going to Karate for about a year now and he seems to like it and do well at it. He has gone to several karate camps on the weekends.

Tell Amy and Julie happy birthday. How is Amy doing at UTA? Will she finished there or go to Austin? What is her major? What is Julie doing? Did she get a masters? Let us know all this stuff. We are all doing just fine

Love

Pat.

Letter's Context

2026-11-1

At that time, we were determined to leave New Zealand but it would have been a shame to pass up the opportunity to see some of the South Island. The two islands, North and South are remarkably different; North is volcanic in origin and geology, South is more like a micro continent, little marked by vulcanism.

wrote Oct. 14, 1988 of party for Mike and Julie, no seeds, no house buyer.

15 Lynmouth Ave..
Karori, Wellington 5, New Zealand

2114 Tierney Ave.
Ft Worth, TX

Dear Eve,

I’m sorry that we can’t come to the party for Mike and Julie – and we were trying to think of something to send – something small enough to send my air. The only thing Walt could think of was jewelry but I told him that Mike didn’t like the last earrings we had sent to him. So we decided to send the money for the cake. That way all of you could share it. Have something funny put on it like – “oh sheep, another birthday” {New Zealand humor, where sheep outnumbered people 2 to 1} Make it a really fancy cake – and enjoy. Let us know how it all turns out.

Stores are all out of seeds right now – but I’ll keep checking.

House still on market – no buyers yet – but the market will pick up as spring warms up. Right now we are being buffeted by strong Equinox-ical winds just about every day.

Love.
